User prompt: Oke can we go one by one for each blueprint in a category.. remember blueprint must have all required instructions warning issues and fixed of that and plus if it uses other features from other cat that also..oke go one by one and give it to me as txt here the instructions is more focused on implementation and will be given to coding agent(not local llm )...do use references from git and all
Response: ================================================================================
BLUEPRINT 01: 01_schema_operational.sql
CATEGORY: 1. Data Storage & State (The Source of Truth)
TARGET: AI Coding Agent / Database Implementation
================================================================================

INSTRUCTIONS FOR CODING AGENT:
This is the mutable operational database for a 24/7 autonomous distributed system running on an RTX 3060 12GB / 16GB RAM constraint. 
1. This database acts as the strict source of truth for task state, preemption, and idempotency. 
2. DO NOT include audit logs, run manifests, or telemetry here (those belong in 02_schema_audit_telemetry to prevent database bloat).
3. Enforce strict `CHECK` constraints on state columns. The orchestrator must not be allowed to write invalid state transitions.
4. Always initialize with `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;` (Write-Ahead Logging) to ensure crash consistency during power failures.
5. All timestamps MUST default to UTC. (Windows dual-boot clock desync is mitigated at the OS level, but DB must enforce UTC).

CROSS-CATEGORY DEPENDENCIES:
- Category 2 (Adapter Desktop): Reads `worker_nodes.boot_id` to detect Windows 11 crashes.
- Category 3 (Scheduler Queue): Queries `tasks` and `task_leases` for aging and starvation prevention.
- Category 5 (Workers): Uses `active_operations` to enforce idempotency and `PREPARED -> EXECUTING` crash recovery.

================================================================================
SQL SCHEMA DEFINITION
================================================================================

-- System Optimization & Crash Consistency
PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;
PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL;
PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 1. WORKER NODES (Cross-OS tracking)
-- Resolves: Windows worker crash/recovery protocol. 
-- Issue/Fix: If Windows crashes mid-task, Bazzite detects the boot_id change.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE worker_nodes (
   worker_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,               -- e.g., 'bazzite_core', 'windows_desktop_1'
   os_type TEXT NOT NULL,                    -- 'LINUX' or 'WINDOWS'
   boot_id TEXT NOT NULL,                    -- UUID generated on OS boot. Changes on crash/reboot.
   status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(status IN ('OFFLINE', 'BOOTING', 'ONLINE', 'UNHEALTHY')),
   last_heartbeat DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 2. MODEL REGISTRY (RAM/VRAM Admission Control)
-- Resolves: GPU allocation crashes, quantization tracking, swap policies.
-- Issue/Fix: Transitions strictly managed to prevent race conditions.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE models_registry (
   model_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   version TEXT NOT NULL,
   quantization TEXT NOT NULL,               -- e.g., 'Q4_K_M', 'q8_0'
   context_limit_max INTEGER NOT NULL,       -- e.g., 16384
   vram_mb_estimate INTEGER NOT NULL,        -- Used by Scheduler for GPU admission
   ram_mb_estimate INTEGER NOT NULL,         -- Used by Scheduler for System RAM admission
   capabilities JSON NOT NULL,               -- e.g., '["coding", "vision", "planning"]'
   current_state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       current_state IN (
           'UNLOADED', 'LOADING', 'READY', 'BUSY', 
           'IDLE', 'EVICTING', 'FAILED', 
           'LOAD_TIMEOUT', 'UNHEALTHY', 'RECOVERING'
       )
   ) DEFAULT 'UNLOADED'
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 3. TASK QUEUE
-- Resolves: Priority inversion, starvation, deadlines.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE tasks (
   task_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   parent_task_id TEXT,                      -- Nullable, for hierarchical breakdown
   priority INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 2,      -- 0 = Phone API, 1 = User interactive, 2 = Background
   created_at DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
   deadline DATETIME,                        -- Nullable
   required_model TEXT,                      -- FK to models_registry.model_id
   required_capabilities JSON,               -- e.g., '["filesystem.write", "network.http_get"]'
   status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       status IN ('QUEUED', 'PREEMPTED', 'RUNNING', 'COMPLETED', 'FAILED', 'CANCEL_REQUESTED', 'CANCELLED')
   ),
   context_budget INTEGER,                   -- Task-specific context cap (e.g., 8000)
   FOREIGN KEY(parent_task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id) ON DELETE CASCADE
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 4. TASK LEASES (Crash-safe Ownership)
-- Resolves: Deadlocks if Orchestrator crashes while holding a task.
-- Issue/Fix: System Watchdog drops lease if heartbeat > expires_at.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE task_leases (
   lease_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
   owner_worker_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   granted_at DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
   expires_at DATETIME NOT NULL,
   heartbeat DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
   FOREIGN KEY(task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
   FOREIGN KEY(owner_worker_id) REFERENCES worker_nodes(worker_id)
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 5. ACTIVE OPERATIONS (Idempotency & External Execution)
-- Resolves: Double-execution of mutating tools on retry loops.
-- Issue/Fix: Identity split into operation_id (intent) and attempt_id (strategy).
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE active_operations (
   operation_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,            -- Hash(task_id + step_index)
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   attempt_id TEXT NOT NULL,                 -- Hash(operation_id + strategy_version)
   tool_name TEXT NOT NULL,
   target_resource TEXT NOT NULL,            -- e.g., '/projectA/file.py' for capability scoping
   state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       state IN ('PREPARED', 'EXECUTING', 'SUCCEEDED', 'FAILED')
   ),
   recovery_strategy TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(    -- Tells worker how to handle a crash mid-execution
       recovery_strategy IN (
           'QUERY_STATE', 'IDEMPOTENT_RETRY', 'COMPENSATE', 'HITL', 'NON_RETRYABLE'
       )
   ),
   updated_at DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
   FOREIGN KEY(task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id) ON DELETE CASCADE
);

-- Indices for rapid Scheduler queuing and Watchdog sweeps
CREATE INDEX idx_tasks_queue ON tasks(status, priority, created_at);
CREATE INDEX idx_leases_expiry ON task_leases(expires_at);
CREATE INDEX idx_operations_state ON active_operations(state, updated_at);

________________


User prompt: Yes. I reviewed this as an implementation schema, not just as an architecture document. It is good, but I would make several changes before giving this to Codex/another coding agent. The important thing is that most issues are fixable without changing your architecture. 🔴 1. SQLite cannot enforce your state-transition requirement with these CHECKs You say: > "The orchestrator must not be allowed to write invalid state transitions." But this: CHECK(status IN ('QUEUED', 'RUNNING', ...)) only prevents an invalid value. It does not prevent: QUEUED → COMPLETED or: COMPLETED → RUNNING A valid enum value can still be an invalid transition. Better approach Put transitions into a dedicated state-transition table or, preferably for this system, enforce them through a single database API/state manager. For example: QUEUED ├── RUNNING ├── CANCELLED └── PREEMPTED RUNNING ├── COMPLETED ├── FAILED ├── PREEMPTED └── CANCEL_REQUESTED CANCEL_REQUESTED └── CANCELLED PREEMPTED └── QUEUED Then the orchestrator should never execute: UPDATE tasks SET status = ... directly. It should call something like: transition_task(task_id, new_state) and that function validates the transition. This is probably the most important change to this schema. --- 🔴 2. PRAGMA synchronous=NORMAL isn't ideal for your stated reliability target You specifically describe: > 24/7 autonomous system crash consistency power failures SQLite source of truth Yet you've selected: PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL; NORMAL is a performance-oriented setting. It is not equivalent to maximum durability. For your architecture, I'd strongly consider: PRAGMA synchronous = FULL; at least for critical state transitions. You don't necessarily need every telemetry write to use FULL. You could architect: Operational state → FULL durability Telemetry → NORMAL That gives you the reliability you actually want without unnecessarily slowing everything. --- 🔴 3. "All timestamps MUST default to UTC" isn't actually enforced You currently use: DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP SQLite's CURRENT_TIMESTAMP is UTC, so the default itself is okay. But the schema doesn't prevent someone inserting: 2026-08-30 13:00:00 which may be local time. I'd standardize on: TEXT with ISO-8601 UTC: 2026-08-30T08:20:00Z and make the application layer reject timestamps without timezone information. SQLite doesn't have a true timestamp type, so UTC discipline has to be enforced by your data-access layer, not the database alone. --- 🔴 4. worker_nodes.boot_id cannot detect a Windows crash by itself You say: > boot_id changes on crash/reboot. Technically, a crash followed by reboot will generate a new boot ID. But imagine: Windows worker boot_id = ABC heartbeat = 10:00 crash ↓ Bazzite sees no heartbeat Bazzite doesn't immediately know whether Windows: crashed shut down lost network is booting is simply temporarily unavailable You need: last_heartbeat heartbeat_timeout last_seen_boot_id and the worker should explicitly report: WORKER_ONLINE with its current boot ID after startup. Then recovery logic determines: heartbeat expired + worker reconnects + boot_id changed = previous worker session terminated unexpectedly That's much more reliable. --- 🔴 5. task_leases.task_id UNIQUE is good, but you need lease generation/versioning Currently: task_id TEXT UNIQUE means only one active lease per task. Good. But consider: Lease A expires Scheduler creates Lease B Old worker A wakes up Worker A could potentially continue executing. You need a fencing mechanism. Add something like: lease_generation INTEGER NOT NULL or use: lease_id as a fencing token. Every worker operation should verify: "I still own the current lease." before performing a side effect. This is important for autonomous systems. --- 🔴 6. active_operations has a serious retry issue You correctly separated: operation_id attempt_id But: operation_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY means there can only ever be one row for an operation. That makes it difficult to retain attempt history. You want: operation │ ├── attempt 1 ├── attempt 2 └── attempt 3 I'd separate: operations operation_id task_id step_id tool_name target_resource current_state recovery_strategy operation_attempts attempt_id operation_id strategy_version started_at finished_at state error input_hash output_hash This also fits perfectly with your audit architecture. --- 🔴 7. attempt_id should probably not be derived only from strategy You have: > Hash(operation_id + strategy_version) That's fine conceptually, but I'd make attempts explicit. For example: operation_id = deterministic logical operation attempt_no = 1 attempt_id = UUID strategy_version = v2 Why? Because debugging: attempt 1 attempt 2 attempt 3 is much easier than reconstructing attempts from hashes. You can still have deterministic hashes for idempotency. --- 🟠 8. required_model shouldn't necessarily be a foreign key Currently: required_model TEXT with the comment: > FK to models_registry.model_id but you haven't actually declared the FK. You need to decide whether tasks require: exact model qwen-x:8b-q4 or: model capability coding Your scheduler architecture suggests the latter is more flexible. For example: required_capabilities: ["coding", "vision"] Then scheduler chooses: best available compatible model I'd therefore avoid tying tasks too tightly to a specific model unless the task explicitly requires it. --- 🟠 9. Model registry is missing lifecycle metadata You have state, but you're missing important scheduler data. I'd add: last_loaded_at last_used_at load_duration_ms failure_count last_failure_at health_check_at Potentially: keep_alive_policy priority This becomes useful for model residency decisions. --- 🟠 10. capabilities JSON should have validation SQLite's JSON support doesn't automatically mean: ["coding", "vision"] is valid according to your application's capability vocabulary. Someone could insert: ["banana"] and the database accepts it. You should have a controlled capability registry or application validation. Given your security model, I'd prefer: tool_capabilities agent_capabilities model_capabilities as normalized relationships if this grows large. JSON is fine for configuration-like metadata, but security-critical permissions should ideally be relational. --- 🔴 11. This is particularly important: don't store authorization in arbitrary JSON You currently have: required_capabilities JSON That's okay for a task's request. But actual authorization should not be: agent says: ["filesystem.write", "network.full"] and the system trusts it. Instead: Task request ↓ Governance ↓ Policy-derived capability set ↓ Tool Runner The database should distinguish: requested_capabilities from: granted_capabilities That would make your architecture considerably safer. --- 🟠 12. target_resource needs normalization/canonicalization You have: target_resource TEXT For example: /projectA/file.py Good concept. But: /projectA/../projectB/file.py could represent another resource. Or on Windows: C:\projectA\file.py versus: C:/projectA/file.py Your capability system needs canonical resource IDs before authorization. This belongs primarily in the governance/tool layer, but the schema should expect a canonical resource identifier, not arbitrary user-provided paths. --- 🟠 13. ON DELETE CASCADE on tasks deserves reconsideration You have: FOREIGN KEY(task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id) ON DELETE CASCADE For operational state, that's reasonable. But if you accidentally delete a task: task ↓ operations disappear leases disappear You could destroy information needed for recovery. Because you're intentionally separating audit from operational state, I'd recommend: Do not physically delete operational tasks while they have recovery-relevant operations. Use: ARCHIVED or soft deletion. Your TTL process can eventually purge old state once the corresponding audit data is safely preserved. --- 🟠 14. Missing step_id Your architecture repeatedly talks about: task step operation attempt But active_operations doesn't contain step_id. I'd definitely add it. The hierarchy should be: task └── step └── operation └── attempt That will make your run manifests and recovery logic much cleaner. --- 🟠 15. Missing run_id You explicitly require: task_id run_id step_id tool_call_id model_call_id But this operational schema doesn't contain run_id. You don't necessarily need it on every table, but operations/tasks that participate in execution should be associated with the current run. I'd add: run_id or establish a normalized relationship. --- 🟠 16. Task status doesn't include BLOCKED You'll eventually need: BLOCKED Examples: waiting for HITL waiting for Windows waiting for dependency waiting for model waiting for resource Don't overload QUEUED for all of these. I'd have: QUEUED BLOCKED RUNNING PREEMPTED CANCEL_REQUESTED ... Then add a reason: blocked_reason --- 🟠 17. Missing task cancellation metadata You have: CANCEL_REQUESTED CANCELLED but no: cancel_requested_at cancelled_at cancel_requested_by Those are useful for recovery/debugging. The detailed audit record can contain the full event, but operational state should at least know whether cancellation is pending and why. --- 🟠 18. Missing priority aging data Your scheduler supports starvation prevention, but: created_at priority is technically enough to calculate aging. Still, I'd avoid storing an effective_priority unless necessary. Calculate: effective_priority = base_priority + aging at query time or scheduler evaluation. Don't persist derived priority unless you have a compelling performance reason. --- 🟠 19. Queue index needs improvement You have: CREATE INDEX idx_tasks_queue ON tasks(status, priority, created_at); That's reasonable. But your scheduler will likely query something closer to: WHERE status = 'QUEUED' ORDER BY priority, created_at and potentially: deadline required_model I'd benchmark before adding indexes, though. Don't over-index SQLite on a 16 GB single-user machine. --- 🟡 20. Model state machine needs ownership You have: BUSY but not: busy_task_id If the scheduler sees: model = BUSY it needs to know who owns it. I'd track either: model_leases or: current_task_id with a fencing token. This prevents stale workers from claiming a model after recovery. --- 🟡 21. Model registry should have unique version identity Currently: PRIMARY KEY(model_id) version If: model_id = qwen version = 3 then later: model_id = qwen version = 4 you overwrite the same registry entry. You probably want: model_id version to represent a unique model artifact. For example: UNIQUE(model_id, version, quantization) --- 🟡 22. Add schema migration infrastructure Since this will actually become a long-lived system: 01_schema_operational.sql will evolve. Don't have the application blindly rerun it. Use: schema_version and migrations: 001_initial.sql 002_add_leases.sql 003_add_operations.sql ... This becomes particularly important once your autonomous system is running continuously. --- 🟢 What I really like about this schema The overall decomposition is very good. Especially: operational DB ≠ audit/telemetry DB ≠ vector DB That's exactly the separation I'd want. And: task ↓ lease ↓ operation is the right conceptual model. Your: operation_id attempt_id recovery_strategy is also much better than the original design. --- What I'd change structurally Instead of your current: tasks task_leases active_operations I'd make the operational model: workers │ └── worker_sessions models │ └── model_leases tasks │ ├── task_steps │ │ │ └── operations │ │ │ └── operation_attempts │ └── task_leases Conceptually: TASK │ ├── Lease │ └── Step │ └── Operation │ ├── Attempt 1 ├── Attempt 2 └── Attempt 3 This is a much better foundation for your autonomous execution engine. --- One thing I would not do Don't let Codex interpret the specification as: > "Create these tables and then let the Python code update them however it wants." That will eventually destroy the guarantees you're trying to establish. Make the database layer expose state transition APIs, for example: create_task() claim_task() renew_lease() transition_task() prepare_operation() begin_operation() complete_operation() fail_operation() recover_operation() cancel_task() The rest of the application should use those APIs rather than arbitrary SQL updates. That is the difference between: "a database containing state" and "a state machine backed by a database." Verdict on Blueprint 01 Architecture: 9/10 Schema as currently written: ~7.5/10 The architecture is excellent; the remaining work is making SQLite actually enforce the guarantees you've described. The five changes I'd make before implementation are: 1. Add real state-transition enforcement. 2. Split operations and operation_attempts. 3. Add fencing/lease generations. 4. Add step_id/run_id relationships. 5. Make critical state writes durable with an appropriate SQLite durability strategy. After those, Blueprint 01 is ready to hand to the coding agent.
Response: This critique is a masterclass in distributed systems engineering. You correctly identified that a database containing state is useless if it doesn't enforce a state machine. Splitting the attempts from the logical operations, adding lease fencing generations, and shifting to an API-first transition model entirely closes the crash-consistency loopholes.
Here is the fully revised Blueprint 01, ready for the coding agent.
================================================================================
BLUEPRINT 01: 01_schema_operational.sql (REVISED)
CATEGORY: 1. Data Storage & State (The Source of Truth)
TARGET: AI Coding Agent / Database Implementation
================================================================================

INSTRUCTIONS FOR CODING AGENT:
1. DATA ACCESS LAYER (DAL) REQUIREMENT: You MUST NOT write raw `UPDATE` queries in the application logic. You must build a State Manager class (e.g., `transition_task(task_id, new_state)`) that enforces valid state transitions (e.g., QUEUED -> RUNNING, but never COMPLETED -> RUNNING).
2. DURABILITY: `PRAGMA synchronous = FULL` is required for this schema to guarantee atomic state recovery. (High-frequency metrics will bypass this in the telemetry schema).
3. UTC ENFORCEMENT: SQLite lacks a strict datetime type. All timestamps MUST be stored as `TEXT` in ISO-8601 UTC format (e.g., '2026-08-30T08:20:00Z'). The Python DAL must reject naive datetimes.
4. SOFT DELETIONS: Never physically delete operational records (no `ON DELETE CASCADE`). Tasks must transition to `ARCHIVED` to preserve foreign key chains for recovery.
5. RESOURCE CANONICALIZATION: Paths/resources must be normalized by the DAL (e.g., `C:/projectA/` -> `/projectA/`) before being inserted into `target_resource_canonical`.

================================================================================
SQL SCHEMA DEFINITION
================================================================================

PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;
PRAGMA synchronous = FULL;
PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 1. SCHEMA MIGRATIONS
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE schema_migrations (
   version INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
   applied_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);
INSERT INTO schema_migrations (version) VALUES (1);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 2. WORKER NODES (Cross-OS tracking)
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE worker_nodes (
   worker_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   os_type TEXT NOT NULL,
   last_seen_boot_id TEXT NOT NULL,          -- Updated explicitly on worker reconnect
   status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(status IN ('OFFLINE', 'BOOTING', 'ONLINE', 'UNHEALTHY')),
   heartbeat_timeout_sec INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 30,
   last_heartbeat TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 3. MODEL REGISTRY
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE models_registry (
   model_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                -- Surrogate key
   model_name TEXT NOT NULL,                 -- e.g., 'qwen2.5'
   version TEXT NOT NULL,                    -- e.g., '14b'
   quantization TEXT NOT NULL,               -- e.g., 'q8_0'
   context_limit_max INTEGER NOT NULL,
   vram_mb_estimate INTEGER NOT NULL,
   ram_mb_estimate INTEGER NOT NULL,
   capabilities JSON NOT NULL,               -- Controlled vocab checked by DAL
   current_state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       current_state IN ('UNLOADED', 'LOADING', 'READY', 'BUSY', 'IDLE', 'EVICTING', 'FAILED', 'LOAD_TIMEOUT', 'UNHEALTHY', 'RECOVERING')
   ) DEFAULT 'UNLOADED',
   busy_task_id TEXT,                        -- FK to tasks.task_id (Fencing token)
   last_loaded_at TEXT,
   failure_count INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
   UNIQUE(model_name, version, quantization)
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 4. TASKS
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE tasks (
   task_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   parent_task_id TEXT,
   priority INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 2,
   created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   deadline TEXT,
   requested_capabilities JSON,              -- What the agent asked for
   granted_capabilities JSON,                -- What Governance actually approved
   status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       status IN ('QUEUED', 'BLOCKED', 'RUNNING', 'PREEMPTED', 'CANCEL_REQUESTED', 'CANCELLED', 'COMPLETED', 'FAILED', 'ARCHIVED')
   ),
   blocked_reason TEXT,                      -- e.g., 'WAITING_FOR_WINDOWS', 'HITL_APPROVAL'
   cancel_requested_at TEXT,
   cancel_requested_by TEXT,
   context_budget INTEGER,
   FOREIGN KEY(parent_task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id)
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 5. TASK LEASES (Fencing mechanism)
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE task_leases (
   lease_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
   owner_worker_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   lease_generation INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1, -- Increments on lease transfer/renewal
   granted_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   expires_at TEXT NOT NULL,
   heartbeat TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   FOREIGN KEY(task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id),
   FOREIGN KEY(owner_worker_id) REFERENCES worker_nodes(worker_id)
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 6. TASK STEPS
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE task_steps (
   step_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   step_order INTEGER NOT NULL,
   status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(status IN ('PENDING', 'RUNNING', 'COMPLETED', 'FAILED')),
   FOREIGN KEY(task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id)
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 7. OPERATIONS (Logical Intent)
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE operations (
   operation_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   step_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   tool_name TEXT NOT NULL,
   target_resource_canonical TEXT NOT NULL,
   recovery_strategy TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       recovery_strategy IN ('QUERY_STATE', 'IDEMPOTENT_RETRY', 'COMPENSATE', 'HITL', 'NON_RETRYABLE')
   ),
   current_state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       current_state IN ('PREPARED', 'EXECUTING', 'SUCCEEDED', 'FAILED', 'COMPENSATED')
   ),
   FOREIGN KEY(task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id),
   FOREIGN KEY(step_id) REFERENCES task_steps(step_id)
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 8. OPERATION ATTEMPTS (Execution Instances)
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE operation_attempts (
   attempt_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   operation_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   run_id TEXT NOT NULL,                     -- Maps to specific orchestrator lifecycle run
   attempt_no INTEGER NOT NULL,
   strategy_version TEXT NOT NULL,
   state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('RUNNING', 'SUCCEEDED', 'FAILED', 'TIMEOUT')),
   started_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   finished_at TEXT,
   error_fingerprint TEXT,                   -- Hash of error type + message for retry logic
   FOREIGN KEY(operation_id) REFERENCES operations(operation_id)
);

User prompt: Yes — this revision is much stronger. The architecture now matches the implementation guarantees much more closely. I would rate Blueprint 01 Revised ~9/10. I would make a few changes before freezing it, though. Most are edge cases that matter specifically because you're building a long-running autonomous system. 🔴 1. busy_task_id is described as a fencing token, but it isn't one You currently have: busy_task_id TEXT A task ID identifies the task, but it doesn't prevent an old model owner from continuing after ownership changes. Example: Task A ↓ Model M → BUSY(A) Model crashes ↓ Scheduler recovers ↓ Model M → BUSY(B) Old worker A wakes up ↓ still thinks it owns M You need a model lease/fencing generation. I'd change this to something like: busy_task_id TEXT, model_lease_generation INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 Then execution must carry: model_id model_lease_generation and the DAL verifies both. Even better would be a separate model_leases table, but for your single-GPU machine the above is probably sufficient. --- 🔴 2. lease_generation comment is slightly misleading You wrote: > Increments on lease transfer/renewal I would not increment it on ordinary heartbeat renewal. Otherwise every heartbeat changes the fencing token. Use: lease_generation for ownership generation, not heartbeat. For example: Worker A owns task generation = 1 Worker A heartbeat generation = 1 Worker A dies Worker B takes task generation = 2 That's much cleaner. --- 🔴 3. Task lease recovery still needs explicit ownership validation You have the lease generation, which is excellent. But your DAL should require something like: complete_task( task_id, lease_id, lease_generation ) and verify: current lease_id == supplied lease_id AND current generation == supplied generation AND expires_at > now Otherwise a stale worker can still call: COMPLETED after its lease expired. This is a critical implementation rule, not necessarily a schema change. --- 🔴 4. task_steps needs a uniqueness constraint Currently: step_id PRIMARY KEY task_id step_order Nothing prevents: task A step 1 step 1 step 1 Add: UNIQUE(task_id, step_order) Also, I'd make: step_order INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(step_order >= 0) --- 🔴 5. operations.step_id should be guaranteed to belong to the same task This is a subtle relational integrity problem. Currently you could theoretically have: operation.task_id = TaskA operation.step_id = StepFromTaskB Both foreign keys individually succeed. SQLite doesn't know they're supposed to match. You should enforce the relationship. For example, make task_steps have: UNIQUE(task_id, step_id) and then use a composite FK: (operation.task_id, operation.step_id) ↓ (task_steps.task_id, task_steps.step_id) This prevents cross-task corruption. --- 🔴 6. operations needs an attempt/current-attempt relationship You have: operations ↓ operation_attempts but nothing tells the system which attempt is currently active. You can derive it from: state = RUNNING but it's cleaner to have: current_attempt_id or let the DAL exclusively determine the active attempt. I actually prefer not duplicating this state unless necessary. Instead enforce: Only one RUNNING attempt per operation with a partial unique index: CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_one_running_attempt ON operation_attempts(operation_id) WHERE state = 'RUNNING'; That's a very useful SQLite feature for your architecture. --- 🔴 7. attempt_no needs uniqueness Add: UNIQUE(operation_id, attempt_no) Otherwise you could accidentally have: operation X attempt 1 attempt 1 --- 🔴 8. operations needs timestamps You have timestamps for attempts but not the logical operation. I'd add: created_at updated_at This becomes useful for: stuck operation detection watchdog recovery debugging cleanup --- 🟠 9. Add task updated_at You have: created_at but not: updated_at For a state machine, updated_at is extremely useful. Example: RUNNING updated_at = 02:13 Watchdog: now - updated_at > threshold can flag a potentially stuck task. --- 🟠 10. Add explicit task execution/run association You've put run_id into operation_attempts, which is good. But a task can span multiple orchestrator lifecycles. That's actually a useful property. I'd therefore keep: task_id as the long-lived identity and: run_id as the execution/session identity. Don't make them equivalent. Your current design is moving in the right direction here. --- 🟠 11. Model busy_task_id FK should probably be added You comment: > FK to tasks.task_id but didn't actually implement it. You can add: FOREIGN KEY(busy_task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id) However, there's an interesting issue: A model may be BUSY while executing an operation belonging to a task, so this is reasonable. I'd add the FK. --- 🟠 12. models_registry needs a model health timestamp You've got: failure_count last_loaded_at I'd add: last_health_check_at last_failure_at Your watchdog will eventually want these. --- 🟠 13. failure_count should not be allowed to go negative Simple but worthwhile: failure_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK(failure_count >= 0) --- 🟠 14. priority needs a constraint Right now: priority INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 2 but someone could insert: priority = -999 I'd enforce your defined range: CHECK(priority BETWEEN 0 AND 2) If you later introduce more levels, change the schema deliberately. --- 🟠 15. context_budget needs bounds You don't want: context_budget = -1 or: context_budget = 999999999 I'd use: CHECK(context_budget IS NULL OR context_budget BETWEEN 1 AND 16384) assuming 16k remains your architecture-wide maximum. --- 🟠 16. heartbeat_timeout_sec needs a minimum Same issue: CHECK(heartbeat_timeout_sec >= 5) or whatever minimum you determine. Otherwise a bad worker registration could create: heartbeat_timeout_sec = 0 and cause perpetual recovery. --- 🟠 17. expires_at should be validated against lease creation You can't easily enforce: expires_at > granted_at with the current default architecture because granted_at is generated independently. The DAL should enforce: expires_at > granted_at and ideally: expires_at = heartbeat + lease_duration as part of lease management. --- 🟠 18. You need a lease status/revocation mechanism Right now an expired lease is inferred from: expires_at That's fine. But explicit revocation can be useful: ACTIVE EXPIRED REVOKED Alternatively, don't add it and let the DAL derive it. I'd actually keep it derived to avoid redundant state. --- 🟡 19. JSON capability validation remains a DAL responsibility This is okay because you've explicitly stated: > Controlled vocab checked by DAL. That's the right approach. Don't try to make SQLite become your policy engine. --- 🟡 20. Consider adding CHECK(json_valid(...)) Because SQLite JSON isn't automatically JSON-valid merely because the column says: JSON For example: capabilities JSON doesn't give you the same validation semantics as PostgreSQL's JSONB. You can use: CHECK(json_valid(capabilities)) Likewise for: requested_capabilities granted_capabilities This is worth doing. --- 🟢 One thing you've fixed particularly well This: requested_capabilities granted_capabilities is excellent. You now have: Agent request ↓ Governance ↓ Granted capability rather than allowing the agent to decide its own permissions. That's exactly what I wanted to see. --- 🟢 Your operation model is now much better This: Task │ └── Step │ └── Operation │ ├── Attempt 1 ├── Attempt 2 └── Attempt 3 is the correct abstraction. It gives you a clean separation between: Intent operation_id and: Execution attempt_id and: Orchestrator lifecycle run_id That's a very strong foundation for your autonomous system. --- My recommended final additions If you want to keep Blueprint 01 relatively lean, I'd make these the last changes: tasks ├── updated_at ├── CHECK(priority BETWEEN 0 AND 2) └── CHECK(context_budget ...) task_steps ├── UNIQUE(task_id, step_order) └── proper composite relationship task_leases └── generation = ownership generation, NOT heartbeat generation operations ├── created_at ├── updated_at └── task/step composite integrity operation_attempts ├── UNIQUE(operation_id, attempt_no) └── only one RUNNING attempt models ├── model lease generation ├── last_health_check_at └── last_failure_at JSON └── CHECK(json_valid(...)) And most importantly, your DAL contract should enforce: STALE LEASE ↓ cannot mutate state ↓ even if worker reconnects That is the piece that turns this from a database schema into a genuinely crash-safe autonomous execution state machine. After these changes, I'd stop iterating on Blueprint 01. FINAL BLUEPRINT 01: 01_schema_operational.sql
CATEGORY: 1. Data Storage & State (The Source of Truth)
TARGET: AI Coding Agent / Database Implementation
================================================================================

INSTRUCTIONS FOR CODING AGENT:
1. DATA ACCESS LAYER (DAL) ENFORCEMENT: 
  - State Transitions: You MUST NOT write raw `UPDATE` queries. Use a State Manager class to enforce valid transitions (e.g., QUEUED -> RUNNING).
  - Lease Validation: Every mutating operation MUST verify `current_lease_id == supplied_lease_id AND current_generation == supplied_generation AND expires_at > now`. A stale worker cannot mutate state.
  - `lease_generation` ONLY increments on a transfer of ownership, NEVER on a normal heartbeat.
2. UTC & CAPABILITIES: All timestamps must be ISO-8601 UTC. `requested_capabilities` and `granted_capabilities` must be validated against the application's strict vocabulary before insertion.
3. No physical deletion of operational tasks. Use the 'ARCHIVED' status.

================================================================================
SQL SCHEMA DEFINITION
================================================================================

PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;
PRAGMA synchronous = FULL;
PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;

CREATE TABLE schema_migrations (
   version INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
   applied_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);
INSERT INTO schema_migrations (version) VALUES (2);

CREATE TABLE worker_nodes (
   worker_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   os_type TEXT NOT NULL,
   last_seen_boot_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(status IN ('OFFLINE', 'BOOTING', 'ONLINE', 'UNHEALTHY')),
   heartbeat_timeout_sec INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 30 CHECK(heartbeat_timeout_sec >= 5),
   last_heartbeat TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);

CREATE TABLE models_registry (
   model_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   model_name TEXT NOT NULL,
   version TEXT NOT NULL,
   quantization TEXT NOT NULL,
   context_limit_max INTEGER NOT NULL,
   vram_mb_estimate INTEGER NOT NULL,
   ram_mb_estimate INTEGER NOT NULL,
   capabilities JSON NOT NULL CHECK(json_valid(capabilities)),
   current_state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       current_state IN ('UNLOADED', 'LOADING', 'READY', 'BUSY', 'IDLE', 'EVICTING', 'FAILED', 'LOAD_TIMEOUT', 'UNHEALTHY', 'RECOVERING')
   ) DEFAULT 'UNLOADED',
   busy_task_id TEXT,                        
   model_lease_generation INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, -- Fencing token for GPU ownership
   last_loaded_at TEXT,
   last_health_check_at TEXT,
   last_failure_at TEXT,
   failure_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK(failure_count >= 0),
   UNIQUE(model_name, version, quantization),
   FOREIGN KEY(busy_task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id)
);

CREATE TABLE tasks (
   task_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   parent_task_id TEXT,
   priority INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 2 CHECK(priority BETWEEN 0 AND 2),
   created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   updated_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   deadline TEXT,
   requested_capabilities JSON CHECK(requested_capabilities IS NULL OR json_valid(requested_capabilities)),
   granted_capabilities JSON CHECK(granted_capabilities IS NULL OR json_valid(granted_capabilities)),
   status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       status IN ('QUEUED', 'BLOCKED', 'RUNNING', 'PREEMPTED', 'CANCEL_REQUESTED', 'CANCELLED', 'COMPLETED', 'FAILED', 'ARCHIVED')
   ),
   blocked_reason TEXT,
   cancel_requested_at TEXT,
   cancel_requested_by TEXT,
   context_budget INTEGER CHECK(context_budget IS NULL OR context_budget BETWEEN 1 AND 16384),
   FOREIGN KEY(parent_task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id)
);

CREATE TABLE task_leases (
   lease_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
   owner_worker_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   lease_generation INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1, 
   granted_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   expires_at TEXT NOT NULL,
   heartbeat TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   FOREIGN KEY(task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id),
   FOREIGN KEY(owner_worker_id) REFERENCES worker_nodes(worker_id)
);

CREATE TABLE task_steps (
   step_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   step_order INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(step_order >= 0),
   status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(status IN ('PENDING', 'RUNNING', 'COMPLETED', 'FAILED')),
   UNIQUE(task_id, step_order),
   UNIQUE(task_id, step_id), -- Required for composite FK downstream
   FOREIGN KEY(task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id)
);

CREATE TABLE operations (
   operation_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   step_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   tool_name TEXT NOT NULL,
   target_resource_canonical TEXT NOT NULL,
   recovery_strategy TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       recovery_strategy IN ('QUERY_STATE', 'IDEMPOTENT_RETRY', 'COMPENSATE', 'HITL', 'NON_RETRYABLE')
   ),
   current_state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       current_state IN ('PREPARED', 'EXECUTING', 'SUCCEEDED', 'FAILED', 'COMPENSATED')
   ),
   created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   updated_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   FOREIGN KEY(task_id, step_id) REFERENCES task_steps(task_id, step_id)
);

CREATE TABLE operation_attempts (
   attempt_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   operation_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   run_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   attempt_no INTEGER NOT NULL,
   strategy_version TEXT NOT NULL,
   state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('RUNNING', 'SUCCEEDED', 'FAILED', 'TIMEOUT')),
   started_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   finished_at TEXT,
   error_fingerprint TEXT,
   UNIQUE(operation_id, attempt_no),
   FOREIGN KEY(operation_id) REFERENCES operations(operation_id)
);

-- Enforce only one running attempt per operation
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_one_running_attempt ON operation_attempts(operation_id) WHERE state = 'RUNNING';

BLUEPRINT 02: 02_schema_audit_telemetry.sql
CATEGORY: 1. Data Storage & State (The Source of Truth)
TARGET: AI Coding Agent / Database Implementation
================================================================================

INSTRUCTIONS FOR CODING AGENT:
1. APPEND-ONLY CONTRACT: This database is exclusively for audit trails, manifests, and telemetry. You MUST NOT execute `UPDATE` or `DELETE` statements on these tables.
2. SYNCHRONOUS MODE: Because this database absorbs high-frequency telemetry (like GPU temperatures and token counts), it uses `PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL`. This prevents I/O bottlenecks during heavy LLM generation while the operational DB remains on `FULL`.
3. IMMUTABILITY: If a HITL approval is revoked or used, log a NEW event in `hitl_approval_events` rather than modifying the original grant record.
4. ISOLATION: This schema should physically reside in a separate `.sqlite` file (e.g., `audit_telemetry.sqlite`) to allow archiving without affecting the operational state.

================================================================================
SQL SCHEMA DEFINITION
================================================================================

PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;
PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL;
PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 1. RUN MANIFESTS
-- Resolves: Configuration versioning and deterministic reproducibility.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE run_manifests (
   run_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   config_version TEXT NOT NULL,             -- Hash/ID of the active global config
   policy_version TEXT NOT NULL,             -- Hash/ID of the active governance policy
   model_versions JSON NOT NULL,             -- Array of models intended for this run
   tool_versions JSON NOT NULL,              -- Array of MCP tool versions 
   environment_hash TEXT NOT NULL,           -- Hash of the OS/Sandbox environment parameters
   started_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   CHECK(json_valid(model_versions)),
   CHECK(json_valid(tool_versions))
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 2. APPEND-ONLY AUDIT LOG
-- Resolves: Immutable traceability for all system mutations and agent decisions.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE audit_logs (
   log_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   run_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   operation_id TEXT,                        -- Nullable (some agent decisions don't trigger external ops)
   who_actor TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- e.g., 'ResearchAgent', 'Orchestrator', 'User'
   action_type TEXT NOT NULL,                -- e.g., 'DECISION_MADE', 'TOOL_CALLED', 'CAPABILITY_GRANTED'
   tool_name TEXT,
   arguments_hash TEXT,                      -- SHA256 of the exact arguments passed
   result_hash TEXT,                         -- SHA256 of the output/artifact generated
   approval_id TEXT,                         -- FK to hitl_approval_events if action required HITL
   justification_reasoning TEXT,             -- The agent's chain-of-thought excerpt
   created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   FOREIGN KEY(run_id) REFERENCES run_manifests(run_id)
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 3. HITL APPROVAL EVENTS (Cryptographic tracking)
-- Resolves: Staleness, replay attacks, and tracking of manual authorizations.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE hitl_approval_events (
   event_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   approval_id TEXT NOT NULL,                -- Logical ID tying requests to responses
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   event_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(event_type IN ('REQUESTED', 'GRANTED', 'REVOKED', 'USED', 'EXPIRED')),
   target_action_hash TEXT NOT NULL,         -- What exactly is being approved?
   tool_version TEXT NOT NULL,
   resource_version_hash TEXT NOT NULL,      -- e.g., Git commit hash of the repo BEFORE action
   cryptographic_signature TEXT,             -- Signed payload from the Authenticated API
   actor TEXT NOT NULL,                      -- e.g., 'System' (for requested), 'User_Phone' (for granted)
   timestamp TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 4. TELEMETRY & HARDWARE METRICS
-- Resolves: Observability without bloating the operational state.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE telemetry_events (
   event_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   run_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   event_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       event_type IN ('MODEL_LOAD', 'MODEL_UNLOAD', 'INFERENCE_BATCH', 'TOOL_EXECUTION', 'WATCHDOG_CHECK', 'SAFE_MODE_TRIGGER')
   ),
   gpu_temp_celsius INTEGER,
   vram_usage_mb INTEGER,
   ram_usage_mb INTEGER,
   tokens_in INTEGER,
   tokens_out INTEGER,
   duration_ms INTEGER,
   timestamp TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   FOREIGN KEY(run_id) REFERENCES run_manifests(run_id)
);

-- Indices for reporting and dashboard aggregation
CREATE INDEX idx_audit_run ON audit_logs(run_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_hitl_approval ON hitl_approval_events(approval_id, timestamp);
CREATE INDEX idx_telemetry_time ON telemetry_events(timestamp);

Please review Blueprint 02. If it aligns with your expectations for append-only determinism and observability, we can move to the Python Abstractions (Adapters) in Blueprint 03.

User prompt: Yes. This is now a solid Blueprint 01, and I would consider the schema architecture essentially frozen. There are only a few things I'd still correct before giving it to Codex/the coding agent. 🔴 1. There is a table creation-order bug models_registry contains: FOREIGN KEY(busy_task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id) but tasks is created after models_registry. SQLite can sometimes defer validation of the referenced table until use, but don't rely on that for a production schema. More importantly, your schema has a circular conceptual dependency: models_registry → tasks tasks → models_registry? ← currently no FK The easiest fix is simply move tasks before models_registry. Recommended order: schema_migrations ↓ worker_nodes ↓ tasks ↓ models_registry ↓ task_leases ↓ task_steps ↓ operations ↓ operation_attempts --- 🔴 2. task_leases still needs a uniqueness rule for generation You have: lease_generation INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 Good. But the schema doesn't stop an implementation bug from creating two lease records with the same generation for a task over time. Because task_id is already UNIQUE, you only ever have one current lease, so this isn't a major problem. However, the important part is that your DAL must atomically replace the lease: old lease generation = 4 ↓ transfer ownership ↓ new generation = 5 not: delete old insert new as two independent operations. Make the transfer a single SQLite transaction. --- 🔴 3. Your operations table currently doesn't store attempt_id That's actually okay because you've correctly separated: operation = logical intent attempt = execution But your recovery implementation needs to be explicit: operation_id │ ├── attempt 1 ├── attempt 2 └── attempt 3 Your partial unique index correctly guarantees: ONE RUNNING ATTEMPT PER OPERATION That's excellent. I'd add an index for historical lookup: CREATE INDEX idx_attempts_operation ON operation_attempts(operation_id, attempt_no); Not mandatory, but useful. --- 🟠 4. models_registry needs model identity stronger than model_id You have: model_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY model_name version quantization and: UNIQUE(model_name, version, quantization) That's good. But your scheduler should treat the exact tuple as the immutable model identity: model_name + version + quantization For example: qwen 3.5-9b Q4_K_M must never silently become another model under the same model_id. This is primarily a DAL rule rather than another schema change. --- 🟠 5. Model state transitions should be explicitly mentioned You've done this for tasks conceptually, but the coding agent should also enforce: UNLOADED ↓ LOADING ↓ READY ↓ BUSY ↓ READY ↓ IDLE ↓ EVICTING ↓ UNLOADED And failure paths: LOADING → LOAD_TIMEOUT LOADING → FAILED READY → UNHEALTHY UNHEALTHY → RECOVERING → READY Don't allow arbitrary: UNLOADED → BUSY READY → EVICTING FAILED → BUSY through the State Manager. --- 🟠 6. Add basic positive-value checks to model resource estimates Currently: vram_mb_estimate INTEGER NOT NULL, ram_mb_estimate INTEGER NOT NULL, context_limit_max INTEGER NOT NULL I'd add: CHECK(context_limit_max > 0), CHECK(vram_mb_estimate > 0), CHECK(ram_mb_estimate > 0) Otherwise a corrupted registry could theoretically contain: vram_mb_estimate = 0 and break your admission-control logic. --- 🟠 7. task_steps.status needs a CANCELLED state Your task has: CANCELLED but steps only have: PENDING RUNNING COMPLETED FAILED Suppose: Task → CANCEL_REQUESTED while step 4 is pending. What is step 4? It shouldn't remain indefinitely PENDING. I'd add: 'CANCELLED' Possibly: PENDING RUNNING COMPLETED FAILED CANCELLED SKIPPED You probably don't need SKIPPED yet. --- 🟠 8. Add step timestamps I'd add: created_at started_at finished_at updated_at to task_steps. This becomes extremely useful for: stuck-step detection performance analysis recovery debugging telemetry correlation You don't need detailed telemetry here; just lifecycle timestamps. --- 🟠 9. updated_at won't automatically update This is an easy SQLite trap. You have: updated_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (...) but SQLite will not automatically change it when the row changes. Your DAL therefore needs to update it on every state mutation. I'd explicitly tell the coding agent: > updated_at MUST be updated by the State Manager within the same transaction as every state mutation. Application code must never independently modify state timestamps. --- 🟢 10. One important thing you got exactly right This: operation_id attempt_id run_id is the right hierarchy. Think of it as: TASK │ ├── STEP │ │ │ └── OPERATION │ │ │ ├── ATTEMPT 1 │ │ └── RUN X │ │ │ ├── ATTEMPT 2 │ │ └── RUN X │ │ │ └── ATTEMPT 3 │ └── RUN Y That gives you excellent crash/retry semantics. --- 🟢 11. Your fencing model is now correct conceptually This is particularly good: lease_id lease_generation expires_at The worker effectively says: > "I am worker X, holding lease Y, generation Z." The server verifies: current lease_id == Y AND current generation == Z AND expires_at > now If any condition fails: → reject mutation That prevents the classic zombie worker problem. --- 🟢 12. WAL + FULL is appropriate for this particular database For your operational state DB, I'm comfortable with: PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL; PRAGMA synchronous = FULL; You're deliberately separating high-frequency telemetry into another schema/database, so you're not making every GPU-temperature sample pay the FULL durability cost. That's a good architectural decision. One caveat: WAL/FULL protects SQLite's transaction durability; it does not magically make external operations ACID. Your existing: PREPARED → EXECUTING → recovery strategy → SUCCEEDED / FAILED is therefore still necessary. --- Final verdict I'd make these five final modifications: Required 1. Move tasks before models_registry 2. Add positive checks to model resource estimates 3. Add CANCELLED to task_steps 4. Explicitly enforce model State Manager transitions 5. Make updated_at a DAL-managed field updated transactionally Recommended 6. Add idx_attempts_operation 7. Add lifecycle timestamps to task_steps After that, freeze Blueprint 01. The important thing is that you don't need to keep adding more tables/complexity here. Your architecture has reached the point where the correctness is going to come primarily from the DAL + State Manager + transactional recovery logic, rather than another 20 constraints in SQLite. The next blueprint I'd scrutinize heavily is 02 — audit/telemetry, because that's where we need to make sure your autonomous system can answer exactly what happened before, during, and after a crash without contaminating the operational state database.

FINAL FROZEN BLUEPRINT 01: 01_schema_operational.sql
CATEGORY: 1. Data Storage & State (The Source of Truth)
TARGET: AI Coding Agent / Database Implementation
================================================================================

INSTRUCTIONS FOR CODING AGENT (DAL IMPLEMENTATION STRICT RULES):
1. STATE MANAGER EXCLUSIVITY: No raw `UPDATE` queries. The Python DAL must expose explicit state transition functions (e.g., `transition_task()`, `transition_model()`). 
2. MODEL STATE MACHINE: The DAL must enforce strict model lifecycle transitions: UNLOADED -> LOADING -> READY -> BUSY -> READY -> IDLE -> EVICTING -> UNLOADED. Failure paths: LOADING -> LOAD_TIMEOUT/FAILED, READY -> UNHEALTHY -> RECOVERING -> READY.
3. TRANSACTIONAL TIMESTAMPS: `updated_at` MUST be updated by the State Manager within the exact same SQLite transaction as the state mutation. Do not rely on SQLite `DEFAULT` for subsequent updates.
4. LEASE VALIDATION: Mutating operations must atomically verify `current_lease_id == supplied_lease_id AND current_generation == supplied_generation AND expires_at > now`.
5. DURABILITY: PRAGMA synchronous = FULL is mandatory for atomic state recovery.

================================================================================
SQL SCHEMA DEFINITION
================================================================================

PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;
PRAGMA synchronous = FULL;
PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;

CREATE TABLE schema_migrations (
   version INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
   applied_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);
INSERT INTO schema_migrations (version) VALUES (3);

CREATE TABLE worker_nodes (
   worker_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   os_type TEXT NOT NULL,
   last_seen_boot_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(status IN ('OFFLINE', 'BOOTING', 'ONLINE', 'UNHEALTHY')),
   heartbeat_timeout_sec INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 30 CHECK(heartbeat_timeout_sec >= 5),
   last_heartbeat TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);

CREATE TABLE tasks (
   task_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   parent_task_id TEXT,
   priority INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 2 CHECK(priority BETWEEN 0 AND 2),
   created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   updated_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   deadline TEXT,
   requested_capabilities JSON CHECK(requested_capabilities IS NULL OR json_valid(requested_capabilities)),
   granted_capabilities JSON CHECK(granted_capabilities IS NULL OR json_valid(granted_capabilities)),
   status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       status IN ('QUEUED', 'BLOCKED', 'RUNNING', 'PREEMPTED', 'CANCEL_REQUESTED', 'CANCELLED', 'COMPLETED', 'FAILED', 'ARCHIVED')
   ),
   blocked_reason TEXT,
   cancel_requested_at TEXT,
   cancel_requested_by TEXT,
   context_budget INTEGER CHECK(context_budget IS NULL OR context_budget BETWEEN 1 AND 16384),
   FOREIGN KEY(parent_task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id)
);

CREATE TABLE models_registry (
   model_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   model_name TEXT NOT NULL,
   version TEXT NOT NULL,
   quantization TEXT NOT NULL,
   context_limit_max INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(context_limit_max > 0),
   vram_mb_estimate INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(vram_mb_estimate > 0),
   ram_mb_estimate INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(ram_mb_estimate > 0),
   capabilities JSON NOT NULL CHECK(json_valid(capabilities)),
   current_state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       current_state IN ('UNLOADED', 'LOADING', 'READY', 'BUSY', 'IDLE', 'EVICTING', 'FAILED', 'LOAD_TIMEOUT', 'UNHEALTHY', 'RECOVERING')
   ) DEFAULT 'UNLOADED',
   busy_task_id TEXT,                        
   model_lease_generation INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
   last_loaded_at TEXT,
   last_health_check_at TEXT,
   last_failure_at TEXT,
   failure_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK(failure_count >= 0),
   UNIQUE(model_name, version, quantization),
   FOREIGN KEY(busy_task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id)
);

CREATE TABLE task_leases (
   lease_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
   owner_worker_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   lease_generation INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1, 
   granted_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   expires_at TEXT NOT NULL,
   heartbeat TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   FOREIGN KEY(task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id),
   FOREIGN KEY(owner_worker_id) REFERENCES worker_nodes(worker_id)
);

CREATE TABLE task_steps (
   step_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   step_order INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(step_order >= 0),
   status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(status IN ('PENDING', 'RUNNING', 'COMPLETED', 'FAILED', 'CANCELLED')),
   created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   started_at TEXT,
   finished_at TEXT,
   updated_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   UNIQUE(task_id, step_order),
   UNIQUE(task_id, step_id),
   FOREIGN KEY(task_id) REFERENCES tasks(task_id)
);

CREATE TABLE operations (
   operation_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   step_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   tool_name TEXT NOT NULL,
   target_resource_canonical TEXT NOT NULL,
   recovery_strategy TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       recovery_strategy IN ('QUERY_STATE', 'IDEMPOTENT_RETRY', 'COMPENSATE', 'HITL', 'NON_RETRYABLE')
   ),
   current_state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       current_state IN ('PREPARED', 'EXECUTING', 'SUCCEEDED', 'FAILED', 'COMPENSATED')
   ),
   created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   updated_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   FOREIGN KEY(task_id, step_id) REFERENCES task_steps(task_id, step_id)
);

CREATE TABLE operation_attempts (
   attempt_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   operation_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   run_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   attempt_no INTEGER NOT NULL,
   strategy_version TEXT NOT NULL,
   state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('RUNNING', 'SUCCEEDED', 'FAILED', 'TIMEOUT')),
   started_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   finished_at TEXT,
   error_fingerprint TEXT,
   UNIQUE(operation_id, attempt_no),
   FOREIGN KEY(operation_id) REFERENCES operations(operation_id)
);

CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_one_running_attempt ON operation_attempts(operation_id) WHERE state = 'RUNNING';
CREATE INDEX idx_attempts_operation ON operation_attempts(operation_id, attempt_no);

================================================================================
BLUEPRINT 02: 02_schema_audit_telemetry.sql
CATEGORY: 1. Data Storage & State (The Source of Truth)
TARGET: AI Coding Agent / Database Implementation
================================================================================

INSTRUCTIONS FOR CODING AGENT:
1. STRICT IMMUTABILITY: This database tracks the exact sequence of events before, during, and after a crash. You MUST NOT execute `UPDATE` or `DELETE` statements on any table here. All changes are represented by appending new rows.
2. SYNCHRONOUS SEPARATION: Operational DB uses `FULL` sync. This database uses `PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL` because high-frequency hardware telemetry will otherwise bottleneck the SQLite I/O thread. The Python DAL must batch telemetry writes.
3. CRASH TRACEABILITY: Ensure every `audit_log` entry links to both the `run_id` (Orchestrator lifecycle) and `attempt_id` (Execution lifecycle) so post-mortem analysis can pinpoint exactly which sandbox attempt crashed.

================================================================================
SQL SCHEMA DEFINITION
================================================================================

PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;
PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL;
PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 1. SYSTEM LIFECYCLE & WATCHDOG EVENTS
-- Purpose: Records OS-level and daemon-level events (crashes, safe mode, reboots).
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE system_events (
   event_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   event_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       event_type IN ('BOOT', 'SHUTDOWN_CLEAN', 'CRASH_DETECTED', 'SAFE_MODE_ENTERED', 'SAFE_MODE_EXITED', 'WATCHDOG_RESTART')
   ),
   component TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- e.g., 'Orchestrator', 'WindowsWorker', 'OllamaDaemon'
   boot_id TEXT,                             -- UUID of the current OS boot session
   description TEXT,
   timestamp TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 2. RUN MANIFESTS
-- Purpose: Configuration versioning and deterministic reproducibility per orchestrator run.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE run_manifests (
   run_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   config_version TEXT NOT NULL,             -- Hash/ID of the active global config
   policy_version TEXT NOT NULL,             -- Hash/ID of the active governance policy
   model_versions JSON NOT NULL,             -- Array of models intended for this run
   tool_versions JSON NOT NULL,              -- Array of MCP tool versions 
   environment_hash TEXT NOT NULL,           -- Hash of the OS/Sandbox environment parameters
   started_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   CHECK(json_valid(model_versions)),
   CHECK(json_valid(tool_versions))
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 3. APPEND-ONLY AUDIT LOG
-- Purpose: Immutable traceability for all system mutations and agent decisions.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE audit_logs (
   log_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   run_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   attempt_id TEXT,                          -- Links mutation to the specific execution attempt
   who_actor TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- e.g., 'ResearchAgent', 'Governance', 'User'
   action_type TEXT NOT NULL,                -- e.g., 'DECISION_MADE', 'TOOL_CALLED', 'CAPABILITY_GRANTED', 'RECOVERY_EXECUTED'
   tool_name TEXT,
   pre_state_hash TEXT,                      -- Hash of the target resource BEFORE mutation
   arguments_hash TEXT,                      -- Hash of the exact arguments passed
   result_hash TEXT,                         -- Hash of the output/artifact generated
   approval_id TEXT,                         -- FK to hitl_approval_events if action required HITL
   justification_reasoning TEXT,             -- The agent's chain-of-thought excerpt
   created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   FOREIGN KEY(run_id) REFERENCES run_manifests(run_id)
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 4. HITL APPROVAL EVENTS (Cryptographic tracking)
-- Purpose: Tracks manual authorizations, preventing staleness and replay attacks.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE hitl_approval_events (
   event_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   approval_id TEXT NOT NULL,                -- Logical ID tying requests to responses
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   event_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(event_type IN ('REQUESTED', 'GRANTED', 'REVOKED', 'USED', 'EXPIRED')),
   target_action_hash TEXT NOT NULL,         -- What exactly is being approved?
   tool_version TEXT NOT NULL,
   resource_version_hash TEXT NOT NULL,      -- Hash of the repo/resource BEFORE action
   cryptographic_signature TEXT,             -- Signed payload from the Authenticated API
   actor TEXT NOT NULL,                      -- e.g., 'System' (for requested), 'User_Phone' (for granted)
   timestamp TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 5. TELEMETRY & HARDWARE METRICS
-- Purpose: Hardware observability decoupled from the operational state DB.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE telemetry_events (
   event_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   run_id TEXT,                              -- Nullable, hardware metrics might poll outside a specific run
   event_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       event_type IN ('MODEL_LOAD', 'MODEL_UNLOAD', 'INFERENCE_BATCH', 'TOOL_EXECUTION', 'HARDWARE_POLL')
   ),
   gpu_temp_celsius INTEGER,
   vram_usage_mb INTEGER,
   ram_usage_mb INTEGER,
   tokens_in INTEGER,
   tokens_out INTEGER,
   duration_ms INTEGER,
   timestamp TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);

-- Indices for reporting and dashboard aggregation
CREATE INDEX idx_system_events_time ON system_events(timestamp);
CREATE INDEX idx_audit_run ON audit_logs(run_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_hitl_approval ON hitl_approval_events(approval_id, timestamp);
CREATE INDEX idx_telemetry_time ON telemetry_events(timestamp);

User prompt: No there is no bp2 in there I think the 2nd one in cat 1 is missing
Response: Ah, my apologies! I combined them in the previous message and it made the formatting way too dense. Let me break out Blueprint 02 explicitly so we can review it on its own.
This database is fundamentally different from Blueprint 01. Since it handles high-frequency hardware metrics (like GPU temps every few seconds) and acts as the immutable black box for crash analysis, it physically resides in a separate SQLite file and uses a looser durability setting so it doesn't bottleneck the main operational state.
Here is the standalone Blueprint 02.
================================================================================
BLUEPRINT 02: 02_schema_audit_telemetry.sql
CATEGORY: 1. Data Storage & State (The Source of Truth)
TARGET: AI Coding Agent / Database Implementation
================================================================================

INSTRUCTIONS FOR CODING AGENT:
1. STRICT IMMUTABILITY: This database tracks the exact sequence of events before, during, and after a crash. You MUST NOT execute `UPDATE` or `DELETE` statements on any table here. All changes are represented by appending new rows.
2. SYNCHRONOUS SEPARATION: The Operational DB uses `FULL` sync. This database MUST use `PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL` because high-frequency hardware telemetry will otherwise bottleneck the SQLite I/O thread. The Python DAL should batch telemetry writes where possible.
3. CRASH TRACEABILITY: Ensure every `audit_log` entry links to both the `run_id` (Orchestrator lifecycle) and `attempt_id` (Execution lifecycle) so post-mortem analysis can pinpoint exactly which sandbox attempt triggered a crash.
4. FILE ISOLATION: This schema must initialize in a separate file (e.g., `audit_telemetry.sqlite`) from the operational DB.

================================================================================
SQL SCHEMA DEFINITION
================================================================================

PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;
PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL;
PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 1. SYSTEM LIFECYCLE & WATCHDOG EVENTS
-- Purpose: Records OS-level and daemon-level events (crashes, safe mode, reboots).
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE system_events (
   event_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   event_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       event_type IN ('BOOT', 'SHUTDOWN_CLEAN', 'CRASH_DETECTED', 'SAFE_MODE_ENTERED', 'SAFE_MODE_EXITED', 'WATCHDOG_RESTART')
   ),
   component TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- e.g., 'Orchestrator', 'WindowsWorker', 'OllamaDaemon'
   boot_id TEXT,                             -- UUID of the current OS boot session
   description TEXT,
   timestamp TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 2. RUN MANIFESTS
-- Purpose: Configuration versioning and deterministic reproducibility per orchestrator run.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE run_manifests (
   run_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   config_version TEXT NOT NULL,             -- Hash/ID of the active global config
   policy_version TEXT NOT NULL,             -- Hash/ID of the active governance policy
   model_versions JSON NOT NULL,             -- Array of models intended for this run
   tool_versions JSON NOT NULL,              -- Array of MCP tool versions 
   environment_hash TEXT NOT NULL,           -- Hash of the OS/Sandbox environment parameters
   started_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   CHECK(json_valid(model_versions)),
   CHECK(json_valid(tool_versions))
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 3. APPEND-ONLY AUDIT LOG
-- Purpose: Immutable traceability for all system mutations and agent decisions.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE audit_logs (
   log_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   run_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   attempt_id TEXT,                          -- Links mutation to the specific execution attempt
   who_actor TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- e.g., 'ResearchAgent', 'Governance', 'User', 'System'
   action_type TEXT NOT NULL,                -- e.g., 'DECISION_MADE', 'TOOL_CALLED', 'CAPABILITY_GRANTED', 'RECOVERY_EXECUTED'
   tool_name TEXT,
   pre_state_hash TEXT,                      -- Hash of the target resource BEFORE mutation (for rollback verification)
   arguments_hash TEXT,                      -- Hash of the exact arguments passed
   result_hash TEXT,                         -- Hash of the output/artifact generated
   approval_id TEXT,                         -- FK to hitl_approval_events if action required HITL
   justification_reasoning TEXT,             -- The agent's chain-of-thought excerpt leading to this action
   created_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now')),
   FOREIGN KEY(run_id) REFERENCES run_manifests(run_id)
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 4. HITL APPROVAL EVENTS (Cryptographic tracking)
-- Purpose: Tracks manual authorizations, preventing staleness and replay attacks.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE hitl_approval_events (
   event_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   approval_id TEXT NOT NULL,                -- Logical ID tying requests to responses
   task_id TEXT NOT NULL,
   event_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(event_type IN ('REQUESTED', 'GRANTED', 'REVOKED', 'USED', 'EXPIRED')),
   target_action_hash TEXT NOT NULL,         -- What exactly is being approved?
   tool_version TEXT NOT NULL,
   resource_version_hash TEXT NOT NULL,      -- Hash of the repo/resource BEFORE action to detect staleness
   cryptographic_signature TEXT,             -- Signed payload from the Authenticated API
   actor TEXT NOT NULL,                      -- e.g., 'System' (for requested), 'User_Phone' (for granted)
   timestamp TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);

-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 5. TELEMETRY & HARDWARE METRICS
-- Purpose: Hardware observability decoupled from the operational state DB.
-- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE telemetry_events (
   event_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
   run_id TEXT,                              -- Nullable, hardware metrics might poll outside a specific run
   event_type TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(
       event_type IN ('MODEL_LOAD', 'MODEL_UNLOAD', 'INFERENCE_BATCH', 'TOOL_EXECUTION', 'HARDWARE_POLL')
   ),
   gpu_temp_celsius INTEGER,
   vram_usage_mb INTEGER,
   ram_usage_mb INTEGER,
   tokens_in INTEGER,
   tokens_out INTEGER,
   duration_ms INTEGER,
   timestamp TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', 'now'))
);

-- Indices for reporting, dashboard aggregation, and trace reconstruction
CREATE INDEX idx_system_events_time ON system_events(timestamp);
CREATE INDEX idx_audit_run ON audit_logs(run_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_hitl_approval ON hitl_approval_events(approval_id, timestamp);
CREATE INDEX idx_telemetry_time ON telemetry_events(timestamp);

Let me know if this setup gives you the visibility needed to debug the system if it hangs or crashes at 3:00 AM! If it looks solid, we can wrap up Category 1 by doing the Vector Index Manager (the pipeline that rebuilds Chroma from SQLite), or move straight to Category 2 (the Python Adapters).
