User prompt: Yes — this is now close to a genuinely frozen BP06. The architecture is consistent with BP01–BP05. I would make four final corrections before giving this exact version to Codex/Coding Agent. 1. The RAM formula currently double-counts the OS reserve You have: RAM_REQUIRED = MODEL_RAM_ESTIMATE + KV_CACHE_RAM + RUNTIME_RAM_OVERHEAD_MB + CURRENT_SYSTEM_USAGE + OS_RAM_RESERVE_MB If CURRENT_SYSTEM_USAGE already includes the OS, the additional 4096 MB reserve is potentially double-counting it. For a hard guarantee, I'd define: RAM_REQUIRED = CURRENT_SYSTEM_USAGE + MODEL_RAM_ESTIMATE + KV_CACHE_RAM + RUNTIME_RAM_OVERHEAD_MB + OS_RAM_RESERVE_MB RAM_REQUIRED <= 16,384 but explicitly define CURRENT_SYSTEM_USAGE as measured usage excluding the reserved 4 GB safety allocation, or alternatively remove OS_RAM_RESERVE_MB and make the measured free-RAM threshold the reserve. I prefer the first approach because the 4 GB reservation becomes an explicit architectural invariant. --- 2. "Exact KV cache" needs one important qualification The blueprint says: > Calculate KV_CACHE_VRAM/RAM based on context_budget and q8_0 sizing. That's good, but context length alone does not determine KV-cache size. KV-cache memory depends on model architecture, particularly: number of KV heads head dimension number of layers KV data type context length architecture-specific details So BP01 should contain enough model metadata to calculate it, e.g.: kv_heads head_dim num_layers Then the adapter can calculate the KV requirement instead of guessing. For a GQA model, for example, the conceptual calculation is proportional to: KV bytes ≈ 2 × num_layers × num_kv_heads × head_dim × context_budget × bytes_per_KV_element with the appropriate runtime-specific accounting. Don't hard-code a universal "MB per 1K tokens" multiplier. --- 3. Add model architecture metadata to models_registry This is the only major BP01 dependency I would explicitly add. Something along the lines of: model_vram_mb_estimate model_ram_mb_estimate num_layers num_kv_heads head_dim kv_cache_type embedding/model architecture metadata Then BP06 becomes deterministic: BP01 model metadata ↓ KV calculation ↓ VRAM admission ↓ RAM admission ↓ Ollama This is substantially safer than trusting an estimate supplied by the caller. --- 4. Make the lease check stronger than the example SQL Your comment currently shows: UPDATE models_registry SET current_state='BUSY' WHERE model_id=? AND model_lease_generation=? AND current_state='READY'; But the specification correctly says the task lease must also be verified in the same transaction. So the actual State Manager operation should conceptually be: BEGIN IMMEDIATE verify: task_id task_lease_id task_lease_generation task lease expiry model_id model_lease_generation model state == READY atomically: model state READY → BUSY bind operation/attempt to the model lease COMMIT If any check fails: ROLLBACK ↓ ModelLeaseError ↓ NO Ollama request That is the actual fencing guarantee. --- One state-machine addition I'd make Your current load path says: UNLOADED → LOADING I'd explicitly prevent another worker from loading the same model concurrently: UNLOADED │ │ admission passes ▼ LOADING │ ├── timeout → LOAD_TIMEOUT ├── error → FAILED └── success → READY And the transition must be conditional: UNLOADED → LOADING only if the BP01 state is still UNLOADED. That prevents two model-load requests from racing. --- Final BP06 verdict Architecture: ✅ Freeze Implementation spec: 🟡 Freeze after the four clarifications above The resulting stack is now nicely separated: BP01 Authoritative operational state │ ├── Task lease ├── Model lease ├── Model state ├── Model memory metadata │ ▼ BP06 Model Adapter │ ├── Admission controller ├── Lease fencing ├── Context enforcement └── Telemetry correlation │ ▼ Ollama daemon │ ▼ RTX 3060 / 12 GB And the important architectural rule remains: > Ollama executes. BP01 decides. BP02 records. That is the right boundary for the local AI OS you're building.
Response: You are spot on. Relying on a generic "MB per 1K tokens" multiplier is exactly how systems suffer OOM crashes when switching model architectures (e.g., from standard attention to Grouped Query Attention). The memory math must be intrinsically tied to the exact tensor geometry of the model, and the state transitions must be strictly serialized to prevent race conditions.
Here is the definitively frozen Blueprint 06, including the explicit tensor-based KV calculation, the RAM double-counting fix, the concurrent-load lock, and the absolute transactional fence.
# ==============================================================================
# FINAL FROZEN BLUEPRINT 06: 06_adapter_model.py
# CATEGORY: 2. Core Abstractions & Adapters (The OS Bridge)
# TARGET: AI Coding Agent / Python Backend Implementation
# ==============================================================================
# INSTRUCTIONS FOR CODING AGENT:
# 1. BP01 SCHEMA PATCH: You MUST add `num_layers`, `num_kv_heads`, and `head_dim` (integers) 
#    to the BP01 `models_registry` table. The adapter relies on these for deterministic KV math.
# 2. DETERMINISTIC KV MATH: KV cache size is calculated via tensor geometry, not guessing:
#    KV_bytes = 2 * num_layers * num_kv_heads * head_dim * context_budget * bytes_per_element
#    (For q8_0, bytes_per_element = 1).
# 3. RAM ADMISSION FIX: Do not double-count the OS reserve. The check must be:
#    (CURRENT_MEASURED_USAGE + MODEL_RAM_ESTIMATE + KV_CACHE_RAM + RUNTIME_RAM_OVERHEAD_MB) <= (16384 - OS_RAM_RESERVE_MB)
# 4. CONCURRENT LOAD LOCK: The State Manager transition UNLOADED -> LOADING must be conditional:
#    `UPDATE models_registry SET current_state='LOADING' WHERE model_id=? AND current_state='UNLOADED'`.
#    If 0 rows update, another worker won the race; abort this load.
# 5. ATOMIC INFERENCE FENCE: The BP01 transition to `BUSY` must occur inside a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` 
#    transaction that verifies both the task lease (ID, generation, expiry) AND the model lease 
#    (generation, state == READY). If any check fails, ROLLBACK and raise `ModelLeaseError`.
# ==============================================================================

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from typing import Dict, Any, Optional
from dataclasses import dataclass

class ModelAdmissionError(Exception):
   pass

class ModelLeaseError(Exception):
   pass

@dataclass
class InferenceIntent:
   task_id: str
   step_id: str
   operation_id: str
   attempt_id: str
   task_lease_id: str
   task_lease_generation: int
   model_id: str
   model_lease_generation: int
   prompt: str
   system_prompt: Optional[str]
   context_budget: int
   inference_timeout_sec: int
   temperature: float = 0.0

class ModelAdapter(ABC):
   
   def __init__(self):
       # Hard limits for i5-14400F / RTX 3060 12GB / 16GB RAM architecture
       self.MAX_VRAM_MB = 12288
       self.MAX_RAM_MB = 16384
       
       # Explicit Reserves & Overheads
       self.OS_VRAM_RESERVE_MB = 1536       
       self.RUNTIME_VRAM_OVERHEAD_MB = 512  
       self.OS_RAM_RESERVE_MB = 4096        # This becomes the threshold ceiling
       self.RUNTIME_RAM_OVERHEAD_MB = 1024  

   @abstractmethod
   def verify_backend_config(self) -> bool:
       """
       MUST verify daemon config ensures `q8_0` KV cache on startup.
       """
       pass

   @abstractmethod
   def evaluate_admission(self, model_id: str, context_budget: int) -> bool:
       """
       1. Query BP01 `models_registry` for vram/ram estimates AND tensor geometry:
          `num_layers`, `num_kv_heads`, `head_dim`.
       2. KV_CACHE_BYTES = 2 * num_layers * num_kv_heads * head_dim * context_budget * 1 (for q8_0)
          KV_CACHE_MB = KV_CACHE_BYTES / (1024 * 1024)
       3. Enforce VRAM ceiling:
          if (MODEL_VRAM + KV_CACHE_MB + RUNTIME_VRAM_OVERHEAD_MB) > (MAX_VRAM_MB - OS_VRAM_RESERVE_MB):
              return False
       4. Enforce RAM ceiling:
          if (CURRENT_MEASURED_USAGE + MODEL_RAM + KV_CACHE_MB + RUNTIME_RAM_OVERHEAD_MB) > (MAX_RAM_MB - OS_RAM_RESERVE_MB):
              return False
       5. Return True.
       """
       pass

   @abstractmethod
   def load_model(self, model_id: str, context_budget: int) -> bool:
       """
       1. Query admission. If fail -> raise ModelAdmissionError.
       2. BP01 Fence: UNLOADED -> LOADING (fails if already LOADING).
       3. API Call to Ollama.
       4. BP01 Transition: LOADING -> READY (or LOAD_FAILED / LOAD_TIMEOUT).
       """
       pass

   @abstractmethod
   def generate(self, intent: InferenceIntent) -> Dict[str, Any]:
       """
       1. Atomic Fence via BP01 State Manager (BEGIN IMMEDIATE): 
          Verify task lease (ID, generation, expiry > now).
          Verify model (generation, state == READY).
          UPDATE state to BUSY.
          COMMIT. (If any fail, ROLLBACK -> raise ModelLeaseError).
       2. Execute Backend Inference (with timeout).
       3. Post-Inference State: BUSY -> READY (or FAILED).
       4. Append BP02 `INFERENCE_BATCH` telemetry (blocking INSERT with identifiers).
       """
       pass

   @abstractmethod
   def unload_model(self, model_id: str) -> bool:
       """
       State Machine: READY -> EVICTING -> UNLOADED
       Forces backend drop. Appends BP02 telemetry.
       """
       pass

User prompt: lets go CUA sentinel
Response: CUA-Sentinel it is. It perfectly captures the strict, watchful, boundary-enforcing nature of the architecture we’ve built.
With the storage and execution boundaries locked down in Categories 1 and 2, we are now entering Category 3: Governance & Orchestration. This is the brain of CUA-Sentinel.
We’ll start with Blueprint 07: 07_scheduler_queue.py. The biggest mistake in local AI scheduling is trying to build an in-memory queue (like Celery or Redis) alongside the database. In CUA-Sentinel, BP01 is the only queue. The Scheduler's job is simply to evaluate BP01, calculate starvation-preventing priority, match capabilities, and atomically bind a task to a worker lease.
Here is the frozen blueprint for the Scheduler.
# ==============================================================================
# FINAL FROZEN BLUEPRINT 07: 07_scheduler_queue.py
# CATEGORY: 3. Governance & Orchestration (The Brain)
# TARGET: AI Coding Agent / Python Backend Implementation
# ==============================================================================
# INSTRUCTIONS FOR CODING AGENT:
# 1. NO IN-MEMORY STATE: Do not maintain Python `Queue()` or lists of pending tasks. 
#    BP01's `tasks` table (where status = 'QUEUED') is the absolute and only source of truth.
# 2. STARVATION PREVENTION (PRIORITY AGING): A Priority 2 (Low) task must not wait forever 
#    if Priority 0 (Critical) tasks keep arriving. The scheduler must calculate an "Effective 
#    Priority" that boosts older tasks based on `(now_utc - created_at)`.
# 3. CAPABILITY ROUTING: The scheduler must compare the task's `requested_capabilities` 
#    against the polling worker's declared capabilities (e.g., Windows UI access, GPU access). 
#    If the worker lacks the capability, the task is skipped for that worker.
# 4. ATOMIC TASK CLAIM: Granting a task to a worker requires a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction:
#    Verify task is STILL 'QUEUED' -> Transition to 'RUNNING' -> Generate new `task_leases` 
#    record with `lease_generation = 1` -> COMMIT.
# ==============================================================================

import sqlite3
from typing import Optional, List, Dict
from datetime import datetime, timezone
import json

class TaskClaimResult:
   def __init__(self, task_id: str, lease_id: str, lease_generation: int):
       self.task_id = task_id
       self.lease_id = lease_id
       self.lease_generation = lease_generation

class SchedulerQueue:
   def __init__(self, bp01_db_path: str = "01_schema_operational.sqlite"):
       self.db_path = bp01_db_path
       # Configuration for starvation prevention
       self.AGING_BOOST_SECONDS = 3600 # Every 1 hour of waiting acts like a +1 priority boost

   def _calculate_effective_priority(self, base_priority: int, created_at_iso: str) -> float:
       """
       Base Priorities: 0 (Critical), 1 (High), 2 (Standard/Low). Lower is better.
       Math: Effective Priority = Base Priority - (Wait Seconds / AGING_BOOST_SECONDS)
       """
       created_at = datetime.fromisoformat(created_at_iso.replace("Z", "+00:00"))
       wait_seconds = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - created_at).total_seconds()
       
       boost = wait_seconds / self.AGING_BOOST_SECONDS
       return float(base_priority) - boost

   def claim_next_task(self, worker_id: str, worker_capabilities: List[str]) -> Optional[TaskClaimResult]:
       """
       Invoked when a worker node is IDLE and asks for work.
       1. Queries BP01 for all tasks where status = 'QUEUED'.
       2. Filters out tasks requiring capabilities the worker doesn't have.
       3. Sorts remaining tasks by `Effective Priority` (lowest float value wins).
       4. Attempts an ATOMIC claim on the winning task.
       """
       # Step 1: Read-only query to find candidates
       # Coding Agent: SELECT task_id, priority, created_at, requested_capabilities FROM tasks WHERE status='QUEUED'
       candidates = [] # Mocked
       
       valid_candidates = []
       for row in candidates:
           req_caps = json.loads(row['requested_capabilities']) if row['requested_capabilities'] else []
           # Step 2: Capability check
           if all(cap in worker_capabilities for cap in req_caps):
               eff_pri = self._calculate_effective_priority(row['priority'], row['created_at'])
               valid_candidates.append((eff_pri, row['task_id']))
               
       if not valid_candidates:
           return None
           
       # Step 3: Sort by effective priority (Lowest float wins)
       valid_candidates.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
       best_task_id = valid_candidates[0][1]

       # Step 4: Atomic Claim Transaction
       return self._execute_atomic_claim(worker_id, best_task_id)

   def _execute_atomic_claim(self, worker_id: str, task_id: str) -> Optional[TaskClaimResult]:
       """
       BEGIN IMMEDIATE
           SELECT status FROM tasks WHERE task_id = ?
           if status != 'QUEUED': ROLLBACK, return None
           
           UPDATE tasks SET status = 'RUNNING', updated_at = NOW() WHERE task_id = ?
           
           INSERT INTO task_leases (lease_id, task_id, owner_worker_id, lease_generation, expires_at)
           VALUES (?, ?, ?, 1, NOW() + 5 minutes)
       COMMIT
       """
       # Coding Agent: Implement the strictly fenced SQLite transaction here.
       pass

   def yield_task(self, task_id: str, task_lease_id: str, worker_id: str) -> bool:
       """
       Voluntary yield (e.g., worker needs to reboot or gracefully shutdown).
       Verifies lease. Deletes `task_leases` row. Transitions BP01 task back to 'QUEUED'.
       """
       # Coding Agent: Implement atomic yield transaction.
       pass

Reviewing BP07
This setup ensures that a background task (like archiving files) will eventually beat out a high-priority task if it has been starved for too long, guaranteeing system progress. It also completely eliminates race conditions when multiple workers (e.g., the Bazzite daemon and the Windows desktop client) poll for work simultaneously.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART I: CORE ARCHITECTURAL PRINCIPLES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. THE AUTHORITY TRIAD:
  - Operational Truth (BP01): What is true right now. Authoritative state, atomic fencing.
  - Historical Truth (BP02): What happened. Immutable audit logs, crash records, telemetry.
  - Knowledge Truth (BP03): What the system knows. Canonical SQLite with a disposable Chroma vector projection.

2. HARD BOUNDARIES:
  - Agents never execute raw shell commands. They request capabilities.
  - Governance issues cryptographically signed `CapabilityGrant` tokens.
  - Execution is sandboxed with strict OS-level RAM, CPU, PID, Disk, and Network isolation.
  - VRAM and System RAM admission is mathematically deterministic based on tensor geometry, not estimates.

3. DETERMINISTIC RECOVERY:
  - Idempotency keys (`operation_id`, `attempt_id`) track all executions.
  - Workers trace `boot_id` to detect mid-execution reboots and orphan abandoned tasks.

       self._cleanup_orphaned_rebuilds()

BLUEPRINT 04: 04_adapter_sandbox.py

import os
import re
from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from typing import Dict, List, Optional
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
import enum

class NetworkProfile(enum.Enum):
   NONE = "NETWORK_NONE"
   DNS_ONLY = "NETWORK_DNS_ONLY"
   HTTP_GET_ALLOWLIST = "NETWORK_HTTP_GET_ALLOWLIST"
   FULL = "NETWORK_FULL" 

@dataclass
class CapabilityGrant:
   grant_id: str
   task_id: str
   lease_id: str
   lease_generation: int
   network_profile: NetworkProfile
   allow_gpu: bool
   workspace_id: str
   policy_version: str
   issued_at: datetime
   expires_at: datetime
   ram_limit_mb: int = 1024
   cpu_cores: float = 1.0
   pid_limit: int = 64
   disk_quota_mb: int = 512
   signature: str = "" 

class SandboxExecutionResult:
   def __init__(self, exit_code: int, stdout: str, stderr: str, duration_ms: int, timed_out: bool):
       self.exit_code = exit_code
       self.stdout = stdout
       self.stderr = stderr
       self.duration_ms = duration_ms
       self.timed_out = timed_out

class SandboxAdapter(ABC):
   def __init__(self, host_workspace_root: str):
       self.host_workspace_root = os.path.realpath(host_workspace_root)
       self._active_sandboxes = {} # {sandbox_id: {task_id, lease_id, lease_generation}}

   def _verify_signature(self, grant: CapabilityGrant) -> bool: pass

   def _resolve_and_verify_workspace(self, workspace_id: str) -> str:
       if not re.match(r'^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$', workspace_id): raise ValueError("Invalid format")
       target_path = os.path.realpath(os.path.join(self.host_workspace_root, workspace_id))
       if os.path.commonpath([self.host_workspace_root, target_path]) != self.host_workspace_root:
           raise PermissionError("Path traversal escape detected")
       return target_path

   @abstractmethod
   def create_sandbox(self, grant: CapabilityGrant, base_image: str) -> str: pass
   @abstractmethod
   def execute_command(self, sandbox_id: str, task_id: str, lease_id: str, lease_generation: int, command: List[str], timeout_sec: int) -> SandboxExecutionResult: pass
   @abstractmethod
   def destroy_sandbox(self, sandbox_id: str) -> bool: pass

class WindowsDockerImpl(SandboxAdapter): pass
class BazzitePodmanImpl(SandboxAdapter): pass

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from typing import Dict, Any, Optional
from dataclasses import dataclass

class ModelAdmissionError(Exception): pass
class ModelLeaseError(Exception): pass

@dataclass
class InferenceIntent:
   task_id: str
   step_id: str
   operation_id: str
   attempt_id: str
   task_lease_id: str
   task_lease_generation: int
   model_id: str
   model_lease_generation: int
   prompt: str
   system_prompt: Optional[str]
   context_budget: int
   inference_timeout_sec: int
   temperature: float = 0.0

class ModelAdapter(ABC):
   def __init__(self):
       self.MAX_VRAM_MB = 12288
       self.MAX_RAM_MB = 16384
       
       self.OS_VRAM_RESERVE_MB = 1536       
       self.RUNTIME_VRAM_OVERHEAD_MB = 512  
       self.OS_RAM_RESERVE_MB = 4096        
       self.RUNTIME_RAM_OVERHEAD_MB = 1024  

   @abstractmethod
   def verify_backend_config(self) -> bool: pass

   @abstractmethod
   def evaluate_admission(self, model_id: str, context_budget: int) -> bool:
       """
       KV_CACHE_BYTES = 2 * num_layers * num_kv_heads * head_dim * context_budget * 1
       VRAM_REQ = MODEL_VRAM + KV_CACHE_MB + RUNTIME_VRAM_OVERHEAD_MB <= (MAX_VRAM_MB - OS_VRAM_RESERVE_MB)
       RAM_REQ = CURRENT_MEASURED_USAGE + MODEL_RAM + KV_CACHE_MB + RUNTIME_RAM_OVERHEAD_MB <= (MAX_RAM_MB - OS_RAM_RESERVE_MB)
       """
       pass

   @abstractmethod
   def load_model(self, model_id: str, context_budget: int) -> bool: pass

   @abstractmethod
   def generate(self, intent: InferenceIntent) -> Dict[str, Any]:
       """
       ATOMIC FENCE: BEGIN IMMEDIATE -> Verify task lease & model state -> UPDATE to BUSY -> COMMIT.
       Must write synchronous BP02 `INFERENCE_BATCH` telemetry before returning.
       """
       pass

   @abstractmethod
   def unload_model(self, model_id: str) -> bool: pass

class OllamaModelImpl(ModelAdapter): pass
